Welcome to Milwaukee County



Milwaukee County’s goal is to enrich your life by providing essential services that meet your needs and those of your family, neighbors, co-workers and friends.

Residents



Milwaukee County is home to over 950,000 people living in one of 19 communities, which range in size from the City of Milwaukee, with 595,000 residents, to the Village of River Hills, with roughly 1,600 residents.

Businesses



Still a manufacturing stronghold, the region features 16 Fortune 1000 companies and thousands of others in the financial services, medical device, hospitality and retailing industries.

Municipality Reporting Status



There are 19 municipalities in Milwaukee County. Nine municipalities process their absentee ballots at a central location within the municipality. Absentee ballots for the other ten municipalities are processed at their respective polling locations.



The following municipalities use Central Count of Absentee Ballots (Wis. Stat. Sec. 7.52). Once the County Clerk/Election Commission’s office receives the transmission of 100% of total ballots cast in-person at all polling locations within each of these specific municipalities, the website will indicate that 100% of the reporting units have reported.
 However, results are NOT complete until the county has received the reporting of all ballots, to include ballots processed at central count location.
 A checkmark indicates that the County Clerk/Election Commission’s office has received 100% of ballots processed (in-person ballots AND absentee ballots processed at central count) for that municipality.

To determine which municipalities use central count, and whether the county has received 100% of a specific municipality’s unofficial results, see the 
municipality status
 above.  This race is not complete until the unofficial results from the reporting units (in-person and absentee) that make up this contest are received.  To see the reporting units that make up this specific contest, click the 
Expand/Collapse Totals by Wards
 button below.
    
	
The number of registered voters displayed are as of the day before the election.  In Wisconsin, state law allows voters to register on election day, and as a result, it is possible for a ward to have over 100% participation.
